Two things deliberately did not move: the **file dialog**, because no file under `libs/` imports a Tauri plugin and `ProfilePhotoStore` already settled that the page picks a path and the store takes it from there; and the **two English sentences**, because they are text - the stores publish counts and the page writes words. Those sentences are still hardcoded English beside a correctly translated toast, unchanged here and now filed. `tracklist-import.ts` takes the pure parts - fence stripping, the parse that fails with a truncated excerpt of what came back, the snake_case mapping - none of which could be tested without the component before. **Checked on a rendered screen through the real controls**: the unclaimed row hidden by default and restored by its checkbox with the dashed ANALYSED chip, a padded wrong-case search matching, a minimum score of 0 excluding the unscored row rather than treating null as zero, the Score header sorting both ways, and both write paths failing for real - a failed delete **keeping the confirmation open**, because closing it would claim the row was gone while it is still there. The component fell 334 -> 140 lines; the template grew 275 -> 285 non-empty, still under budget, since the store prefixes make expressions wrap.

- **The welcome screen migrates, and its store holds nothing at all.** Allowlist 9 -> 8. `first-launch` is 439 lines and looked like the hardest of the one-call tier; 417 of them are the inline template and its keyframes, and the class is 22 - one settings write and an output. There is no screen state to move, because the screen has none. The lint rule is about the gateway rather than about signals, so `FirstLaunchStore` takes the call and nothing else; inventing state for it to hold would have been worse than leaving it small. Folding the write into `HealthCheckStore` was refused because that conflates the check with the flags and Settings would inherit a method meaning nothing there, and a shared flags store for `onboarding` to reuse later was refused as designed for a caller that does not exist yet. **The deliberate silence is preserved**: the store records a failed write and returns false, and the screen ignores the result, because a user whose preference could not be saved should still not be trapped on the welcome screen. **Checked on a rendered screen**, where the write was driven with the database genuinely unreachable: the error was recorded, the screen still emitted its intent and unmounted, and the health panel drew the failing check as a single row with "Re-run check" rather than as an error state. Reading it also turned up a gap worth naming: **`app.ts` injects the gateway and the lint rule cannot see it**, because the rule matches `*.component.ts` - so the allowlist has been undercounting the work by one all along. Filed on the ADR checklist, not fixed here.

- **Analytics migrates, and the interesting number is how little was allowed to move.** Allowlist 10 -> 9. Its domain math was already in `libs/core` - `computeAnalytics` with its own spec - so `AnalyticsStore` owns only its two inputs, the facts and the selected period, and hands back the view. Ten computeds stayed on the page because every one of them translates: tile labels, stage names, the leakage sentence, bucket captions, outcome groups. That split is the correct outcome rather than a shortfall; a larger store here would have been a store that translates. **A real bug went with it**: `computeAnalytics(f, period, new Date())` ran _inside_ a computed, so the moment the 30d/90d window was measured from was re-read on every recompute, including every period switch - a window boundary that shifts underneath the comparison, invisible to every gate and to a screenshot. `now` is stamped at load now, the way `DashboardStore` already did it, and a test asserts it does not move across two period switches. The sparkline geometry became `analytics-view.ts` - `polylinePoints` and `areaPoints`, seven tests, including that both series scale against a shared `yMax` so the followups line stays comparable - while the Intl date formatting stayed on the page, since locale is the page's. A failed read now records `error` and installs empty facts so the empty state renders rather than a blank page, and the page raises the same toast it always did. **Checked on a rendered screen** in three parts: the failure path (real error recorded, `Could not load analytics` shown, store silent), the geometry with real facts (13 points from x=0 to x=100, monotonic, peaks on the top inset, area closing on the baseline, the DOM `polyline` carrying the computed string), and the decision itself (`now` identical across `90d -> 30d -> all` while buckets went 13 weekly to 30 daily). The component fell 297 -> 268 lines; its 426/300 template is untouched pre-existing debt.

- **The shell migrates, and half of what it was loading turned out to be dead.** Allowlist 11 -> 10. `ShellStore` owns the settings read and the sidebar rail preference, including its `localStorage` persistence - the first browser-storage access in `libs/application`, taken deliberately because a rail preference is screen state that happens to outlive the session, and reading it at construction is what makes the rail correct at first paint rather than a frame later. **`aiMode` is deleted rather than moved.** It was written on every launch and read nowhere: the sidebar AI indicator it fed left the template after `af4a90a`, and only the spec's mock still mentioned it. Moving dead state down would have published it in the layer's public API and then owed it a test. **The store does not apply the locale**: `setLocale` is an i18n side effect, so the store exposes the stored `uiLanguage` and the shell applies it. Taking the setter as an argument - the shape `CvPrintStore` uses for its normalizer - was offered by the problem and refused for the reason recorded last time: passing a side effect in as a parameter does not make it state. A failed read fills `error` and returns false while the shell keeps its defaults and says nothing, which is unchanged behaviour with the failure no longer invisible. Eight store tests; the component's own spec needed no change at all - the first time this campaign - because it stubs the gateway at the `TestBed` level and the component-scoped store resolves it through the same injector. **Checked on a rendered screen**, where the missing Tauri context gave the failure path for free: the real error recorded, the language left null, English kept, console empty. The rail measured 240 -> 64 with labels hidden and icons kept, and survived a reload - though the first measurement read 240 in every state, taken mid-transition, which is the trap this campaign has now hit three times. The component fell 232 -> 203 lines.

- **The dashboard migrates, and what stayed behind is the clearest statement yet of where the boundary runs.** Allowlist 12 -> 11. `DashboardStore` owns the cards, the jobs overview, the profile, the two values resolved at load because they reach the database, the four KPIs, the new-user test and the upcoming-interview rows. **The action queue deliberately did not move.** Each of its items carries a lucide icon, translated strings and a `run` closure that navigates or opens a modal - view, i18n and routing, three things this layer has refused every time it was asked. It would have been easy to move behind a disguise, taking a label function and an icon map as arguments the way `recentClaimedJobs` already takes its label; that disguise is worth naming, because **passing view concerns in as parameters does not make them stop being view concerns**. `dashboard.util.ts` moved down whole with its spec, since it was already pure and splitting a 104-line file to hand over three helpers is what was rejected for the onboarding gate util. **And one duplicate was deliberately left standing:** `monogram` is a third copy of the initials idea, but it and `companyInitials` differ on the empty company - the board draws `-`, the dashboard draws `?`. Folding would change what the dashboard renders with nothing behind it but tidiness, so a comment now says they are two rules that look alike rather than one rule written twice. Thirteen tests, including that a failed load leaves the page empty rather than half-populated, that unclaimed jobs do not count towards new-user detection, and that an unfinished tailoring session on an analysed-but-unsaved job still names the job it reopens. **Checked on a rendered screen**, where the non-fold is what showed: KPIs at 4/2/1/1, the upcoming list drawing `GL` at `5h` marked soon, and the empty-company row drawing **`?`** - the preserved difference visible rather than merely asserted. The component fell 449 -> 314 lines as a side effect.

- **The quick-view modal migrates, and the previous PR's extraction pays off one week early.** Allowlist 13 -> 12. `QuickViewStore` owns the comments, the stage lists, the busy flags and all five gateway calls. **The card stayed on the component for two reasons**, and the second is new: it is a required input, _and_ the board mutates those card objects **by reference** when a status or priority changes - so a copy inside the store would be one the store could not keep in sync, and the two would disagree silently. Every method takes the id it needs. **`pickCurrentStage` was the most valuable thing here and had no test:** its comment claims it mirrors the SQL in `db_pipeline_cards` exactly, and if the two drift, the modal's headline and the board's card footer disagree about the same application - which reads as data corruption rather than as a bug in a predicate. It now sits in `interview-stage-view.ts` beside the store with seven tests, including the case the SQL exists for: rejected and cancelled stages are ignored while anything is still open, but a fully closed funnel still shows the furthest it got. **Two duplicates were folded rather than moved:** the modal's own `initials()` and `scoreClass()` were second copies of the functions extracted for the board one PR ago, and the comment on the first said "matching the board card" - a claim kept true by hand. They call the shared functions now, so the claim is structural. Twenty-four tests, three of which pin decisions: a failed comment write keeps what the user typed, a space-only comment never reaches the gateway, and an unset priority compares equal to `null` so clicking "none" on a card that never had one is not a write. **Checked on a rendered screen**: `AC` and `score--high` rendering through the shared functions, the stepper drawing reached/done/current correctly at `2/3`, and all three write paths - a failed comment keeping `Worth keeping` in the box, a successful one clearing it, and the status write emitting the whole row to the board rather than the status literal.

- **The Kanban board migrates, and it is the first store here whose data is not a signal.** Allowlist 14 -> 13. `PipelineStore` owns the board, the search, the collapsed archive columns, the selected card and all three gateway calls. **`cards` stays a mutable `Record<status, PipelineCard[]>`, and moved in that shape on purpose**: CDK's `transferArrayItem` and `moveItemInArray` mutate the arrays they are handed, and the drop handler undoes a failed move by calling `transferArrayItem` back the other way. Rebuilding that around immutable updates is a change to the board's core interaction with its own risk and its own verification, and bundling it into a migration is how a refactor becomes an outage - so the conversion stays available as its own decision, now with a real test suite behind it. **The revert stayed on the component**, because it is CDK's operation on CDK's event and the store has no business knowing what a `CdkDragDrop` is: the store reports whether the write succeeded, the page puts the card back. Four pure functions - `companyInitials`, `stageTotal`, `stageSegments`, `scoreClass` - moved _beside_ the store rather than onto it, the way `tracker-columns` already does; two of them encode rules rather than formatting (a progress track must never be shorter than the stage the card reached, an unscored card must not read as low-scoring) and as plain functions they are testable without a component. A stale comment was corrected on the way: it claimed the mutable model works "under default change detection", but the component has been `OnPush` since it was written - it renders because `OnPush` marks a component dirty on its own event handlers, which is a different fact with a different failure mode. Twenty-eight tests, including that a failed status write leaves the card untouched so the board cannot show a move the database refused, and that a failed stage check does not produce a prompt on an application that may already have stages. **Checked on a rendered screen, the most thorough of this campaign:** three active columns and two rails, the extracted initials rendering `AC`/`GL`/`IS`/`UM`, all three score bands, search narrowing to `1 match`, and both drop paths - a failed write returning the card to its column at index 0 with order and status intact, and a successful one mirroring `followUpAt` from the written row, deriving `overdue` from it, recounting the total, and opening the quick-view with the card selected.

- **Interview Prep's list page migrates whole, and a swallowed error was caught in the draft rather than in review.** Allowlist 15 -> 14. `InterviewPrepStore` owns the cards, the three computeds over them, the open row menu, the delete confirmation and `removing`. **The menu and the confirmation moved with the data, and that does not reverse the earlier decision to leave `tracker`'s on the page**: that one had a mechanical reason - its row component is reused rather than recreated as the selection changes, so a child owning the flag would carry one row's half-confirmed delete to the next. This is a page, destroyed on navigation, and the reason does not reach it. A rule and its reason are different things, and it is the reason that travels. `stats` returns `nextAt` as the stored ISO string and `formatDate` stayed on the component, because formatting is locale-dependent and this layer refuses translations for the same reason it refuses toasts. **The caught regression:** the first draft of the store swallowed both failure paths with a bare `catch { return false }` - it compiles, it passes lint, and it would have downgraded the page's toast from the real error text to whatever generic wording the component invented. The store keeps an `error` signal instead, the third to share that contract, and refusing stays distinguishable from failing by whether it is set. Twelve tests, three of which pin decisions rather than mechanics: unscheduled rows sink below scheduled ones because a date the user has not set should not outrank one they have; a removed row is cleared in place rather than by reloading, because a reload would re-sort every other row under the user; and a **failed** delete leaves the row in the list, because the list must not lie about what was removed. **Checked on a rendered screen**: four cards in, three rows out, sorted 01 Sept before 10 Sept with the unscheduled last, stats reading 3 and 3, and `01 Sept 2026` rendered by the component from the store's raw date. The row menu still lights via `aria-expanded`, so the design-system rule survives the state move, and a live failed delete left all three rows in place and toasted the real error.

- **A date-locale defect was found and filed, not fixed here.** `en-GB` is hardcoded at five call sites - `pipeline`, `quick-view-modal` twice, `interview-prep` and its detail page - while the app ships six locales, so five of them render dates in a format their user did not ask for. It needs four files this migration does not touch and a rendered check per locale, so it gets its own pull request.

- **The profile photo migrates, and it is the first state this layer could not take.** Allowlist 16 -> 15. `ProfilePhotoStore` owns `saving`, `cropSourceUri`, `error` and the two gateway calls - read a picked file into a data URI, persist the cropped result. **`uri` deliberately stayed on the component**: it is a `linkedSignal` seeded from a _required input_, an input belongs to the component that declares it, and a store cannot derive one. Both alternatives were worse than the constraint - reproducing the link with a `seed()` method and an effect is more machinery than the thing it replaces, and adds a re-seed ordering question that does not exist today. So the component keeps the value it renders and the optimistic set-and-revert around it. **The `saving` guard is in both, and that is not duplication**: the store's copy protects the gateway from any caller, while the component's protects the optimistic write - without it a second click would show the new photo _before_ the store refused to write it, and the revert would put back a value the first save is still replacing. The store also does not choose between the "saved" and "removed" wordings, because those are translations and translations are the app's - the same boundary now drawn three times. Nine tests, including that a refused second write never reaches the gateway and that a file the backend cannot read leaves the crop modal closed rather than opening a crop over nothing. **Checked on a rendered screen**, where the thumb first measured 2px wide and looked like a broken frame: the card was mid-collapse and the read caught the transition. Computed style is authoritative and says 90 x 120, `object-fit: cover`, radius 8, image loaded at its natural 80 x 80 - `getBoundingClientRect` is no more free of animation than `getComputedStyle` is, which is amendment twenty-three's lesson arriving from a new direction.

- **Both print windows move to stores, allowlist 18 -> 16, and moving the state is what exposed a duplicate nobody had seen.** `cv-print` and `cover-letter-print` are the hidden Tauri windows the WYSIWYG PDF export loads - two gateway calls each, read the document and tell Rust the snapshot may be taken. `CvPrintStore` and `CoverLetterPrintStore` own the render signals and the load, and both return `false` for a missing row rather than throwing, which keeps the behaviour the components had: the window stays blank and the Rust side times out, because printing a default letter would hand the user a PDF they did not ask for. **The interesting part was not the migration.** Both components carried a `signalReady()` that was **byte-identical** - the same fonts wait, the same two settle ticks, the same `document.body.classList.add('printing-cv')`, down to the same comment - and nothing made that visible while each file was 100 lines of load-and-render. Moving the load out left the duplicate as the only thing remaining in each: **extracting state exposes duplication that size was hiding.** It is now one `awaitPrintSettle()`, and it deliberately did **not** move next to the stores - every line of it touches `document.fonts` and `document.body`, and the application layer owns state, not view timing; putting it there would have been the first DOM access in that layer and a precedent easier to set than to withdraw. `normalizeCvContent` is passed into `CvPrintStore.load` rather than imported by it, following the precedent `CvDocumentStore` already documents, because moving it would mean moving a 652-line util that 25 files import. Eighteen tests: sixteen across the two stores, plus one that the body is marked printable and one that a `document.fonts.ready` which never resolves is given up on rather than hanging the export. **Checked on a rendered screen**: both routes render at 793.7 x 1143.9 - A4 at 96dpi, 210mm exactly - fed from their stores, and the cover letter rendered `09.08.2026` from `language: 'de'`, which is the specific drift its comment warns about.

- **Two more migrations, allowlist 20 -> 18, and the rendered check found a defect the migration did not cause.** `health-check-panel` (156 lines) and `stage-quick-add` (83) were the next-cheapest entries by call site - one gateway call each. `HealthCheckStore` got its own `libs/application/health/` area rather than joining `onboarding/`, because the panel has two homes, the first-launch screen and Settings, and naming the area for either is wrong at the other; `run` takes the label for its failure row, since this layer holds no translations, and a check that cannot answer stays a `fail` row rather than becoming an error state. `StageQuickAddStore` took the **whole form** and not only the write: `busy` and `error` are what ADR-0005 calls "what is in flight", so leaving the three draft fields on the component would have split one screen's state across two owners. The toast deliberately stayed behind - `submit` returns the stage or `null` and leaves `error` set, and refusing stays distinguishable from failing, because a refusal (an empty label, or a save already running) leaves `error` empty and says nothing. Twelve tests across the two stores, on behaviour that had none: neither component had a spec. **The rendered check then found something else.** Driving the health panel through its states showed all three status icons computing the same colour, `rgb(244,242,237)`: the `[class]="'health-item__icon health-item__icon--' + i.status"` binding lands nothing, the rendered `<lucide-icon>` has an empty `classList`, and so `--ok` green, `--warn` amber and `--fail` red have never applied. `git diff main` on that file touches no line of the binding, so it is pre-existing and is filed separately rather than fixed in a migration PR - the likely cause is `lucide-angular`'s own host `class` binding winning, which would make the pattern inert wherever else it appears. Three consecutive rendered checks have each now found something no gate reports.

- **The gateway allowlist drops 22 -> 20, and one of the two was never work at all.** With the icon-button campaign closed, `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY` is the job again. Ranking the 22 entries by **how much gateway each actually uses** rather than by whether it injects one found `paste-job-modal.component.ts` injecting `DbService` and never calling it - the field was the only occurrence of `db` in the file, so that entry had been overstating the remaining migration work since it was written. Three lines, no behaviour. The real migration is deliberately the smallest one available: `onboarding-banner.component.ts`, 69 lines, one gateway read pair, deciding one boolean. `OnboardingBannerStore` owns `visible`, `load()` and `dismiss()`, and opens a new `libs/application/onboarding/` area beside discover, documents and tracker. **One boolean is the smallest state this layer has owned; the size is not the point, the read is** - the banner had to call `getSettings` and `getProfile` to decide whether to show, which is the only reason the component held a gateway. One consequence was forced rather than chosen and will recur: a store in `libs/application` **cannot import from the app**, so `shouldShowOnboardingBanner` had to come down with it - together with `shouldAutoOpenOnboarding` and their shared spec, as one unit, because splitting the file would have left one gate concept with two homes and a spec cut in half. `OnboardingService` deliberately stayed in the app and so did the half of `finishSetup` that uses it: the store hides the nudge, the component asks the shell to reopen the wizard. Five tests cover the store, including the one behaviour nothing pinned anywhere before - a failed read **hides** the banner rather than rejecting, because a banner is not worth an error state. **Checked on a rendered screen**: 976 x 48 at radius 8, `role="status"`, both `appButton` controls intact, Dismiss clearing the element, and Finish setup opening the wizard end to end across the new boundary. Four documents recorded the allowlist as 26 long after it was 22; all of them now say 20.

- **The icon-button campaign closes: the last page-local copy is gone, and the reason it was kept had expired.** `.clb__icon-btn` in `cover-letter-body-paragraphs/` was named locally in amendment seventeen for a specific reason - folding it _then_ meant a visible change, because `variant="danger"` coloured at rest. Amendment nineteen made the destructive variant quiet at rest again, so that reason was spent; the question was whether a **different** one had replaced it, and the measurement is what answered it rather than the argument. Rest state and geometry were already the design system's on every property - 28px box, `--radius-input`, 1px transparent border, `--text-secondary`, the same 140ms `background` transition - and `--space-2` at 4px around a 13px icon lands exactly on `min-width: 28px`, so the box does not move. **The only delta was hover**: the local rule painted a neutral `--surface-hover` plate with a red icon, where the design system paints `--danger-tint` with a `--danger` ring. What settled it was `git show` on the four rules the fold had already replaced - `.ipd__icon-btn--danger:hover` was tint, ring and colour, verbatim the design system's - so this button was not preserving a considered local look, it was the **one destructive icon in the app that disagreed with every other one**. It folds onto `appButton variant="danger" size="icon"`; `quality:style-move` reads **3 selectors lost, 0 gained**, the second pure deletion in a row and the last one this campaign owed. **Checked on a rendered screen in both themes**, transitions frozen: 28.00 x 28.00 at radius 6 at rest and on hover in each, `rgb(154,150,140)` dark and `rgb(90,90,99)` light at rest, `rgba(224,86,86,0.14)` with a `rgb(240,122,122)` ring dark and `rgba(224,86,86,0.1)` with `rgb(224,86,86)` light on hover. The hover was measured by retargeting the design system's own `:hover` rule onto a probe class in the CSSOM and restoring it - the real declarations through the real cascade, since a duplicated copy would only have proved that the copy works. A test pins the three classes, because dropping either attribute breaks nothing that fails: the button still deletes, it just stops looking destructive and loses its box.

- **The last six page-local icon buttons fold, and this time the fold is a pure deletion.** `.jt-icon` is gone from `tracker`, and with it the three shapes that had made it local: the kebab's **30x30 at radius 7**, the drawer's **24px** `--sm`, and a filled variant on `--text-accent` (indigo-**400**) with a hardcoded `#fff`. All six sites are `appButton size="icon"` now - five ghost and one primary - and **no local rule survives anywhere**, which is the first fold in this campaign that leaves nothing behind. `quality:style-move` reports **8 selectors lost, 0 gained**, and 0 gained is the number that matters: a fold that adds nothing local is one the next reader cannot be surprised by. Four declared visual changes, each decided rather than discovered: the kebab is 28x28 at radius 6, the delete-custom-column button grows 24 -> 28, the save button's fill moves indigo-400 -> indigo-600 and its `brightness(1.08)` hover becomes `--accent-hover`, and disabled controls take the system's `not-allowed` over `default`. The kebab's `.is-open` class went the way `interview-prep`'s did - `.btn--ghost[aria-expanded='true']` reads the attribute the trigger already had to carry, so the class is gone from a second page and the rule that was written for one consumer now has three. **Checked on a rendered screen**, and one thing there is worth recording because it looked like a bug and was not: the open kebab measured a transparent background on every read. The cause was the instrument, not the styling - `.btn` carries a 140ms `background` transition, and every measurement was catching it at t=0. With the transition disabled the value resolves to `--surface-hover` exactly as the rule says. A computed-style read is not a free instrument on a transitioning property.

- **The tracker's page template comes under budget, in five cuts, and the descendant selector that has cost this campaign four components travelled correctly for once.** `tracker.component.html` goes **557 -> 278** against a budget of 300, and its stylesheet **893 -> 455**. That was the whole point: the file-size gate refuses to let an over-budget file grow, so the `.jt-icon` fold could not add its two lines per button until this landed. Out come `tracker-export-modal/`, `tracker-column-drawer/`, `tracker-row-menu/`, `tracker-row-actions/` and `tracker-summary-strip/`. **Three of the five needed no inputs at all**: the four tracker stores are declared in the page's `providers`, so a child rendered inside its template resolves the same instances through the injector - a page whose state lives in stores can be cut anywhere, which is ADR-0005's argument restated as arithmetic. The row menu is the exception and deliberately so: its delete confirmation stays on the page, because the page resets it when another row's menu opens and the component is reused rather than recreated, so owning the flag in the child would carry one row's half-confirmed delete to the next. **The kebab renders 30.00 x 30.00 at radius 7**, which is the interesting number - that shape comes from a `.jt-menu .jt-icon` descendant rule, the exact shape of the trap that has cost this campaign four components, and it works here because its ancestor moved with it rather than living in a shared partial. Two traps were paid on the way: the `prefers-reduced-motion` rule is written with a bare `*`, which encapsulation scopes to the page's own elements, so each animating child needed its own copy or it would have kept animating for a user who asked it not to; and `toggleMenu` read its anchor from `event.currentTarget`, which is only set during dispatch, so the cell emits the trigger **element** instead of the event rather than working by accident. `quality:style-move` reports **lossless** across all six stylesheets. Nine dead icons and two aliases went with the markup that used them, one of which - `ok: CircleCheck` - had already been dead on `main`. Twenty-five tests across the five components. **Checked on a rendered screen** with all of them open at once.

- **The sixth icon-button copy folds, and an open menu now says so in the markup rather than in a class.** `.ip__icon` in `_ip-shared.scss` is gone; both interview-prep call sites are `appButton size="icon" variant="ghost"`. The interesting part is what happened to its `.is-open` state: the trigger already bound `[class.is-open]` and `[attr.aria-expanded]` to the same signal, one line apart, so the class was a duplicate of state the DOM was required to carry anyway - and the half a screen reader could not see was the one doing the styling. `.btn--ghost[aria-expanded='true']` in `libs/ui` reads the attribute instead, which deletes the class from the page and adds no name to the button's public vocabulary. Checked before writing it: **no `appButton` in the app carries `aria-expanded` today**, so the rule has exactly the consumers it is given. It is scoped to ghost rather than to `.btn`, because two page copies is the whole of the evidence and amendment nineteen exists because a variant generalised past its evidence behaved wrongly everywhere it was later adopted. Two rest-state differences were accepted rather than reproduced, and both are visible: `--text-tertiary` becomes the design system's `--text-secondary`, and the hover border is dropped as drift - `tracker`'s `.jt-icon`, the same control in every other respect, never had one, so removing it makes the two pages agree. `:disabled` moves 0.4 to the system's 0.5, as PR B moved 0.35. **Checked on a rendered screen**, in both themes: 28.00 x 28.00 at rest and open, radius unchanged at 6px, the open trigger lit while its neighbour stays quiet, and the modal's close button - an **18px** icon, the size that measured 28 x 29.84 before amendment nineteen - square. A test pins the `aria-expanded` binding, because the button still opens the menu if that binding is ever dropped; it just stops looking open, and nothing else here would fail. **`tracker`'s `.jt-icon` is deliberately not in this change**: its six sites are four different shapes, including a 30x30 trigger reached through a descendant selector and an accent fill on a different indigo, and its template is 573 against a budget of 300, so it needs its own decision and its own cut.

- **The cover letter's settings and availability cards become their own components, and the template comes within eight lines of budget.** `cover-letter-settings-card/` takes the region, the default toggle and the tone and length; `cover-letter-availability-card/` takes the four answers a German posting asks a letter to state. The template goes **406 -> 308** against 300, and the page class **339 -> 318**. Across five extractions the editor has gone **669 -> 308** in markup and **644 -> 318** in code. **Amendment sixteen's checklist was run rather than remembered, and it paid twice.** The settings card needed nothing copied: both its `<select>` elements are covered by complete control rules `_editor-shell.scss` emits globally, `.docedit-meta-row select` and `.docedit-style-grid select`, so losing the page's `.coverdetail select` layer cost them nothing. The availability card needed the copy: `_editor-shell.scss` styles `.docedit-style-grid input` but **not** `.docedit-field input`, and these four fields sit in a plain `.docedit-field` - moved as-is they would have rendered as browser defaults with every gate green, `quality:style-move` included. That is the difference between checking and assuming, and the two cards look identical from the markup. `.coverdetail__checkbox` and its two companions moved outright, since nothing else used them. `quality:style-move` reports 0 lost and 2 gained, both the deliberate copy. 10 tests across the two cards, including that the region writes to the row while tone and length write to the letter - the card's four controls have two different owners.

- **The cover letter's recipient block becomes its own component, and the extraction found a CSS trap that the check cannot see.** `cover-letter-recipient-block/` takes the six DIN 5008 address fields; the template goes **491 -> 406** against a 300 budget. **The interesting part is what nearly broke.** The page styles its form controls with `.coverdetail input:not(...)`, a descendant selector rooted at the page's own element - and Angular's emulated encapsulation stamps that with the page's content attribute, so it matches nothing inside a child component. Moving the six inputs out would have rendered them as **browser defaults**, no background, no border, no padding, with every gate green. **`quality:style-move` passes on this by construction**: it compares the declarations each selector carries, and `.coverdetail input` still exists and still carries all nine. What changed is not the rule but what the rule can _reach_, and a declaration-level diff cannot see that. The rule this adds to the splitting checklist: the classes the moved markup names are only half the audit - also list every descendant or element selector rooted at the page that matched inside the moved region, because those are invisible in the markup. The fix is a **copy rather than a move**, keyed on `.coverdetail__field input` so it stays in the child's scope; the page still needs its own for the controls it kept, and `quality:style-move` reports the two gained selectors, which is what the tool asks to have named. **The three components extracted a day earlier were audited against this and are clean** - the Style card's controls sit inside `.docedit-style-grid`, which `_editor-shell.scss` styles globally with a complete control rule, and the popover's rules had already moved into the global partial. A visual check is still owed on all of it and cannot be run without Tauri IPC.

- **The cover letter editor's Style card and style popover become their own components, and the page class finally lands under budget.** `cover-letter-style-card/` takes the document-wide font, size, weight, colour, page size and margins, the ATS warnings and the reset-all; `cover-letter-style-popover/` takes the per-block override fields. `cover-letter-detail.component.ts` goes **405 -> 337 against a 400 budget - under it for the first time** - and the template **669 -> 491** against 300. **This corrects what was written a day earlier.** ADR-0005 amendment fourteen said the five lines over budget were the 21 read-only aliases and that only cutting the template could remove them; the arithmetic was right and the conclusion was wrong. The aliases are all still there. What was actually left in the page was a second responsibility that had never been named, hiding in plain sight because it was markup rather than state - the four store extractions asked what state the page owned, and nobody had asked what panels it owned. **The styles moved with the markup, and the check proves it.** Angular scopes a component's CSS, so the six classes the moved markup uses - `.coverdetail__style-pop`, `-field`, `-label`, `-reset`, `.coverdetail__custom-badge` and `.coverdetail__spacer` - went into `_cover-letter-controls.scss`, which `styles.scss` already emits globally for this exact reason; `npm run quality:style-move` reports **lossless**. `.coverdetail__spacer` moved even though the page still uses it, because two copies of a rule are two rules that can drift. **Both new components inject `CoverLetterStyleStore` rather than taking inputs**: it is provided on the page, so every child resolves the same instance. The one thing that does travel as an output is the reset-all, because the open-popover key is one-at-a-time across the whole editor and stays the page's. The popover keeps the page's `#stylePopover` template as a three-line wrapper, so `cover-letter-block`'s `TemplateRef` contract is untouched. 35 tests across the two new specs, including the margin clamp - which had never had one, because it lived in a page class.

- **The cover letter editor is off the data gateway, and the allowlist is down to 22.** `CoverLetterAiStore` takes the full-letter draft and the per-block regeneration, `cover-letter-generation.ts` takes the pure decisions underneath them, and `cover-letter-detail.component.ts` stops injecting `DbService` and `AiService` entirely - so its line comes out of `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY`, **26 at the start of this campaign, 22 now**. The rule was probed in both directions rather than assumed: re-adding the injection errors with the ADR-0005 message, removing it again is silent. The page goes **517 -> 405** against a 400 budget, and finishes the four-PR arc at **644 -> 405**. **The five lines over are accounted for**: 21 lines of read-only aliases exist only because the template is 669/300 and the ratchet will not let an over-budget file grow, so prefixing forty-odd bindings with `letter.` is blocked until the template is cut. Without them the class is 384. **Two behaviours are now stated rather than implied.** The user's own answers - tone, length, and the three availability fields German postings require - are carried over a fresh draft rather than taken from the model, because they are choices and not generated text; and regenerating one body paragraph keeps the other paragraphs' cache hashes, because dropping them costs the user a regeneration they already paid for. Both had mutants that survived the first pass for the same reason: the fixture did not carry the field, so "keep" and "overwrite" agreed. **40 of 40 mutants die** after fixing those two. The one thing deliberately left alone is the `|`-joined hash input, which can collide if a field contains a `|`: changing the separator would invalidate every hash in every user's database and force a full regeneration of every letter, so the limit is documented in a test instead of fixed.

- **The cover letter's row moves into the application layer, and the two upsert builders are deliberately not merged.** `CoverLetterDocumentStore` takes the load, the row-level fields the editor changes - label, region, the default flag - and the one write that persists all three stores at once; `cover-letter-detail.component.ts` goes **557 -> 517** against a 400 budget. It injects the content and style stores for the same reason `CvDocumentStore` injects its two: `documentLibraryUpsert` takes a whole record, so three stores editing one letter cannot each save their own slice without clobbering the others. **What is shared is the invariant, not the shape.** `siblingsToUndefault` moves into `document-record.ts` and `cvSiblingsToUndefault` now delegates to it, because "default is per region, and a row never displaces itself" is a rule that is invisible when it breaks - the library simply ends up with two defaults for one region and a later save picks the wrong one. The builders stay apart: a CV row carries `templateId` and `themeId` and a letter carries neither, so a merged builder would take both as optional and stop telling the reader which fields a given document type actually writes. `buildCoverLetterUpsert` omits all three of `templateId`, `themeId` and `isApplicationDraft`, and each omission has a test that fails if it reappears - the last one is what keeps reviewing a letter from the apply wizard from promoting the draft into the Documents library early. **The store never toasts and never navigates** (ADR-0005, amendment three): `save` returns the saved row or `null` for "no write happened", and the page decides what to show. **23 of 24 mutants died on the first pass, and the survivor was a real gap.** Inverting the missing-document guard changed nothing any assertion could see, because the null row then threw one line later and landed in the same `catch`. The difference only appears on a _second_ load: with the guard, a load that finds nothing leaves the document already on screen in place; without it, `doc` is cleared to null first. That is now asserted, and the mutant dies. The page still injects `DbService` for the AI draft and per-block regeneration, so it stays on `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY` until that moves too.

- **The cover letter's style moves into the application layer, and the one thing the two document editors genuinely share is extracted.** `CoverLetterStyleStore` takes the style, the per-block and per-paragraph overrides and the debounced ATS safety check; `cover-letter-detail.component.ts` goes **592 -> 557** against a 400 budget. **The shared piece is fifteen lines and no more**: `document-style-safety.ts` holds the debounce interval and a pure `dedupeStyleNotes`, and `CvStyleStore` now uses it too, dropping from 166 lines to 155. The debounce _itself_ was deliberately left duplicated - scheduling depends on a private timer field per store, so sharing it would need a class with a lifecycle, which is more machinery than six duplicated lines earns, and the module says so in its own header so that nobody later "finishes the job". Page geometry stays on the page, because it clamps through an app-local resolver (ADR-0005, amendment five). Two behaviours preserved rather than tidied: a full reset checks **immediately** and drops any pending check, because a pending one would re-check a style that no longer exists; and the paragraph reindex deliberately does **not** re-run the check, because the overrides move but no style value does, and there is a test asserting that round trip does not happen. **All fourteen mutants died on the first pass**, the first time in this campaign that none survived.

- **The cover letter's content moves into the application layer, and the two editors turn out to share almost nothing.** `CoverLetterContentStore` takes the letter itself - its blocks, its body paragraphs, the tone and length, and the availability and salary answers German postings require; `cover-letter-detail.component.ts` goes **644 -> 592** against a 400 budget. **The handover's warning was right and understated.** `CoverLetterStyle` and `CvStyle` are different types: cover letters have no themes at all, and `CvStyleStore`'s 166 lines are mostly theme machinery. Their `sectionStyles` differ structurally too - the CV's is a closed `CvSectionKey` union, the cover letter's an open `Record<string, …>` because it must key `body_<i>` - so generalizing would widen the CV's type and cost it its exhaustiveness. **The genuinely shared code is about fifteen lines**, the debounced style-safety check and its dedupe, and only that will be extracted. **The paragraph-style reindex is the piece worth having tests for.** Deleting a body paragraph has to shift every `body_<i>` style override above it down one, or an override stops belonging to the paragraph the user set it on - a failure nobody notices until a letter prints with the wrong paragraph in bold. It was thirteen lines inside a page class with no test of its own; it now has ten, and an invariant in its doc comment that replaced a branch which could never fire. **Removing that dead branch retired a mutant**, which is a new and cheerful reading of a falling score: starting the loop one index early used to destroy the override _below_ the removal through that very `else`, and without it the extra iteration is a harmless no-op - the code lost a way to be wrong. **And a behaviour change was caught by the empty-fixture trap for the third time in this campaign**: `hydrate` first swallowed a malformed-JSON failure and opened an empty editor, where the page had thrown and shown its error state, and an empty editor over a letter still on disk is one Save away from replacing it with nothing. Every fixture hydrated an already-empty store, where "reset" and "leave alone" agree. The rule now has a name: whenever a store has a reset path, the fixture must arrive non-empty.
- **The Job Tracker is done: 667 -> 304 lines, and the first page in this campaign to finish under its budget rather than by decision.** `TrackerReportStore` takes the export dialog and its two writes, and `tracker.component.ts` stops injecting `DbService` entirely - so it comes off `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY`, which is now **23**, down from 25 at the start of the phase. `cv-detail` had to stop at 517/400 with preview mode and live selection still on it; `tracker` had no equivalent residue, because everything it held was either state or a document rule. **This is the pull request the `TranslateService` decision was made for.** The Eigenbemuehungen sheet is a document, not app chrome: its language follows the chosen market, so `tFor('de')` prints a German sheet out of an English app. About 150 lines - the CSV builder, the plain-text fallback, the cell formatter, the period label and the report's column labels - could move only because a store is allowed to translate a document it renders. The fit note beside them **stays on the page**, because it names the overflowing columns in the UI language: one column list, labelled twice, in two languages, by two callers. **Risk changed the shape of the tests, not only their number.** This document is submitted to the Agentur für Arbeit, so the CSV is asserted byte for byte - metadata block, blank separator line, RFC 4180 quote doubling, one-based row numbering - and the text fallback as a whole sheet including its padding. `generatedOn` became a parameter rather than a clock read, which is both what makes that possible and a property worth having in a document that gets regenerated. **All 28 mutants die**, among them quotes not doubled, fields not wrapped, rows numbered from zero, the two orientation budgets swapped, and the summary's total and response rate crossed. One survived the first pass and was a real gap: the PDF's fallback content was asserted without an archived row, so building it from the grid's rows instead of the report's would have silently dropped an application from the evidence. Also new: `ReportColumn`, `ReportMarket`, `ReportMode` and `reportFit` moved into `libs/application`, so **a purely presentational component now depends on the layer** for the first time.

- **The Job Tracker's inline row editor moves into the application layer.** `TrackerRowEditorStore` takes the draft, the custom values being typed into it, and the two writes that persist them; `tracker.component.ts` goes **487 -> 444** against a 400 budget, and the page keeps the row menu, the toast and the DOM event the store must not see. Two rules that were buried in one 30-line method now have names and tests: **an emptied cell clears the stored value** rather than writing a blank string (`|| undefined` on all seven editable fields), and **a status write only happens when the status actually changed**, compared against the stored row rather than the draft - the editor offers no blank option, so a falsy draft status means "not touched" and must not overwrite a real one. **The reload is passed into `save` rather than run after it**, which is ADR-0005 amendment six's third and least-preferred shape, used here because `saving` has to stay true for the whole operation including the reload - that is what keeps the save button disabled while the grid repopulates, and returning to the page first would clear it early. **Eighteen of twenty mutants die**, including all four that matter: sending an emptied field as `''`, writing a blank status over a real one, comparing the status against the draft, and dropping the re-entry guard that stops a double click producing two writes. The two survivors are both copies in `start`, one line apart and **not the same finding** - spreading the row is defensive, because that row belongs to the grid's list and nothing edits the draft in place only until someone does; spreading the custom map is redundant, because the function that produced it allocates a fresh object every call. Both kept, and the source now says which is which.

- **The Job Tracker's rows leave the gateway, and the PDF export stops carrying its own copy of the rules.** `TrackerRowsStore` takes the rows, the segment, period and status filters, and the archive and delete writes; `tracker.component.ts` goes **536 -> 487** against a 400 budget. The hidden Tauri window that renders the PDF **loads its own rows** - it is a separate window with only query parameters to go on - so it carried a verbatim second copy of the period window, the day count, the report-row filter and sort, and the summary arithmetic, **with no test on either copy**. Two definitions of "the last three months" is how the sheet and the screen come to disagree about what was exported. There is now one, with two real callers, and `tracker-report-print.component.ts` comes off `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY`: **25 -> 24**, verified from the other side by putting an `inject(DbService)` back and watching `nx lint` fail with the rule's own message. **A `null` was answering two questions.** The store's load first returned `Settings | null`, which conflated a database with no settings row - where the report market should default to international - with a read that failed, where the page had always left the market alone; `loadError` now separates them. The same care caught a smaller one: `confirmRemove`'s "this row has no job" guard sits before its `try`, so `finally` never runs and the delete confirmation stays open, and moving the check into the store alone would have started closing it. **Two mutants survive by design, which is a third outcome worth naming.** `Number.isFinite` in the day count cannot change the result - the only non-finite value reachable is `NaN`, and `NaN >= 0` is already false - and `.slice()` before the report's sort is redundant because `.filter()` already copied. Both are kept, and both now say why in their own doc comments, so the next reader does not repeat the investigation. The other 22 die. The two that were real gaps had fixtures that looked asymmetric and were not: removing a row filters by `id` while deleting by `jobId`, which a one-row fixture cannot tell apart, and the print store's period test asserted the narrowed rows but not the narrowed summary.

- **The Job Tracker's columns move into the application layer, and two of the migration's own rules turn out to have been misstated.** `TrackerColumnsStore` takes the column set, the visibility map, the custom columns and their two gateway writes off `tracker.component.ts`, which goes **667 -> 536** against a 400 budget; the page keeps the panel's open flag and, deliberately, the column labels. Two positions changed on inspection, both through the grilling gate and both recorded as ADR-0005 amendment eight. **A store may inject `TranslateService` after all** - `libs/i18n` is `type:util`, so the boundary always permitted it, and the rule the layer actually needs is about _who the text is for_: a toast or an error message is the layer speaking to the user and stays on the page, while the Eigenbemuehungen sheet is a document the layer renders, in a language deliberately different from the UI's. And **column visibility is not view state**, against the plan this phase inherited: unlike a panel's open flag it merges with gateway-loaded custom columns and feeds five derived values ending in the CSV and the PDF, so leaving it on the page would have left nothing owning the result. **Mutation testing caught a behaviour change that had slipped in and that every test agreed with.** The page's load catch only ever emptied the rows; the store's first draft emptied the custom columns too, and since every fixture loaded into an already-empty store, "keep what you have" and "reset to empty" agreed on all of them. The page reloads its columns after every row save, so the difference is a failed reload deleting columns the user still has. All 14 mutants die now, including that one. The tracker had **no test file at all** before this; it has 48. Two pieces of dead code went with the move: `essentialKeys`, referenced only by its own declaration, and a ternary whose two branches were identical.

- **The CV editor no longer touches the data gateway, and the lint allowlist loses its first line.** `CvRegenerationStore` takes the last two methods that did - `regenerateSection` and `pullFromProfile` - so `cv-detail.component.ts` drops `inject(DbService)` and `inject(AiService)` entirely and comes out of `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY`: **26 -> 25**, the first entry ever deleted from a list that only shrinks. That closes `cv-detail` at **1019 -> 517** against a 400 budget across four pull requests. The removal was verified in both directions rather than assumed - putting an `inject(DbService)` back on the page now fails `nx lint` with the rule's own message, and the file was restored byte-exact afterwards. **A store raises typed errors, it does not phrase them.** `CvNoProfileError` carries no user-facing text and the page picks the wording, which is the missing half of "the layer never notifies": the obvious alternative, throwing `new Error(t('...'))`, would put the localized string in the layer that has no `TranslateService`. The two app-local functions the store needs mid-call are grouped into a named `CvRegenerationCodec` rather than passed one by one - amendment seven records that as the readable form once there is more than one.

- **Pulling personal details from the profile now replaces the section instead of editing it in place.** The old code assigned the seven contact fields **onto** the existing personal-details object and then re-set the sections array to force a re-render. That works only because the array reference changed: the section itself stayed identical, so anything comparing sections by identity - an `OnPush` child taking one as an input, a `track` expression - could not see the edit, and the visible result depended on which of those a template happened to use. `mergePersonalDetails` returns a new section, matching how every other section change in the editor already behaves. This is a behaviour change made deliberately inside a refactor rather than a move, which is why it is listed on its own.

- **The CV row itself now lives in the application layer, and it is the only thing that writes it.** `CvDocumentStore` owns the loaded document, the section list, the label, region and default flag, and the single `documentLibraryUpsert` that persists them - which is why it injects `CvPhotoStore` and `CvStyleStore` rather than sitting beside them: the gateway takes a whole record, so three stores cannot each save their own slice without clobbering the others. Section ordering and the row assembly came out as two pure modules, `cv-section-order.ts` and `cv-document-record.ts`. `cv-detail.component.ts` goes **709 -> 589** against a 400 budget, third of four pull requests on that page. **The store never notifies** (ADR-0005, amendment three): `save` and `confirmSaveTemplate` return their result and let a failure throw, so the toast, the transient "Saved" tick and the apply-wizard hand-back stay on the page - which also keeps the user-facing string out of the layer that has no `TranslateService`. Amendment six records the third shape the campaign has found for code a store may not import: where the style work had the page compute a value and hand it over, `load` needs `normalizeCvContent` **during** an operation the store owns, so it takes it as a named parameter. **A correction to what was written an hour earlier:** this PR does not empty `cv-detail`'s entry in `COMPONENTS_STILL_USING_THE_GATEWAY`. `regenerateSection` and `pullFromProfile` still call `getProfile`, `getSettings` and `hashText`, so the page keeps its gateway injection until `CvRegenerationStore`. Two details in the save path were preserved deliberately and are now pinned by tests that fail if either is "tidied": `isApplicationDraft` is **absent** from the upsert input rather than set from the loaded row, because omitting it is what stops editing a wizard draft from promoting the row into the library early; and the default flag is displaced per **region**, so a default DE CV and a default US CV coexist.

- **A mutation test is only as good as the asymmetry in its fixtures, and this time the fixtures hid two live guards.** `moveCvSection` refuses to move a locked header section and refuses to swap one past another. Deleting either guard left all 46 tests green, which reads as dead code - and nearly got both deleted as such, since `reorderCvSections` re-pins afterwards and would undo the move anyway. It is not dead: on a list whose `order` values have drifted, refusing returns it **verbatim** while falling through **reindexes** it, and that difference is exactly what the page did. Every fixture was already pinned and normalized, so the two paths agreed on all of them. Three cases on a drifted list kill both mutants now - and one of those needed a second look too, because `photo`'s neighbour is also locked, so the neighbour check masked the locked-key check until the case moved `personal_details` down onto `summary` instead. This is the same shape as the `CvPhotoStore` survivors recorded on 2026-08-06, one level subtler: there it was two flags set identically in every fixture, here it is every fixture already being in the normal form the function would produce.

- **A CV's style now lives in the application layer, and the decision that put its cascade rules in `libs/core` was reversed before a line of it was written.** `CvStyleStore` holds the `CvStyle`, the theme it sits on, the theme baseline that "Custom" and "Reset" are measured against, and the debounced ATS safety check - its one gateway call. The scope routing that maps a live-style-panel change to the right write target became `routeCvStyleChange`, a pure `(style, selection, change) => CvStyle` beside the other `CvStyle` helpers. `cv-detail.component.ts` goes **962 -> 709** against a 400 budget, the second of four pull requests on that page. **ADR-0005 amendment four sent the cascade to `libs/core` as a new `cv/` directory; reading the code first said no.** Its only input is `CvStylePanelChange` - the wire format of one widget - which amendment two's own "domain or format" test puts on the format side, and the eight helpers it delegates to were already a pure 355-line module in the app, shared with the cover-letter editor and re-exported through `cv-content.util.ts`. Promoting the cascade meant exporting a panel's emission shape as core vocabulary and moving nine import sites, or splitting a module whose header states why its functions belong together. **The general lesson is recorded as amendment five: a store owns state, not necessarily the code that edits it.** `type:application` may not import from the app, so a store cannot call an app-local helper - the page composes the next style and commits it through `applyStyle`. Page geometry stayed on the page for the same reason: clamping margins runs through the app-local `resolvePageSettings`. Two smaller corrections came with it - the save-template trio never reads `style` and moves to `CvDocumentStore`, and the page keeps its `DbService` injection (and its allowlist entry) until that store lands. Both new modules are mutation-tested: four mutations each, all killed, chosen to be invisible by eye - a dropped bullets-only flag, a duplicated near-identical comparison branch, a swapped entry-rule property, a reset that quietly drops page geometry, and a cancelled timer that would have let a stale safety check overwrite a fresh one.

- **The CV editor's photo and personal-detail toggles move into a store, and reading `load` and `save` changed the plan for the other three.** `CvPhotoStore` owns whether a CV shows a photo, where, and whether it carries a birth date or marital status - four toggles that were seven signals and three methods on a 1019-line page class. The image itself is not in it: one reusable photo lives on the profile, and what remains per-document is whether to show it. The section-list side is a pure module beside the store (`readPhotoFlags`, `withPhotoSection`, `applyPhotoFlags`) rather than in `libs/core`, because these are not rules about what a CV means - they are how four editor toggles are read out of a section list and written back into one. `cv-detail.component.ts` **1019 -> 962**. **What the implementation turned up:** `documentLibraryUpsert` writes a **whole record**, so the three planned stores cannot each save their own slice without clobbering the others, and `load()` is a single `Promise.all` that hydrates all three at once. The shape that follows is a `hydrate`/contribute protocol - this store takes its four toggles from a loaded document and returns the sections its toggles change - with the store that owns the row as the only writer, injecting the slice stores when it assembles the upsert. Twenty-four tests, seven mutations, **two survived the first pass**: crossing `includeBirthdate` with `includeMaritalStatus` (every existing case set the two the same way, so the swap was invisible), and "always ensure a photo section" (the switch-off case already had one, so creating one when none existed changed nothing observable). Both closed with the asymmetric cases they were missing.

- **Discover is free of the data gateway, and the rule that unblocks every other page is decided: the application layer never notifies the user.** `DiscoverSourcesService` was the last holdout - it raises seven toasts, and every store written so far returns its failure and lets the page speak, because `ToastService` is app-level by construction and a store reaching for it would make the whole layer depend on the app. It is now `DiscoverSourcesStore` in `libs/application`, and the four writes report `{ ok, error? }` rather than announcing anything. **Three outcomes, not two**, which is why the shape is a record and not the `string | null` the other stores use: a form that refuses its own empty input is neither a success to announce nor a failure to explain, and `null` would have meant both "created" and "nothing to do" - so the drawer would clear a form the user was still filling in. The Sources drawer raises all seven notifications, because it already owned all four call sites; the page is untouched by the change. `resultLine` and `formatScanTime` went with the store on the `scan-console.ts` precedent - pure formatting for one store lives beside it - and `discover-sources.util.ts` moved whole rather than being split, so the page and drawer import `toggled` and `narrowBuiltinsByMarkets` back through the barrel. **Components injecting `DbService`: 45 -> 44**, and no file under `pages/discover/` reaches the gateway any more. The three other services that toast (`job-actions`, `portal-answers`, `cover-letter-tailor` - 7 calls between them) convert when their own pages are migrated; the audit that found them also found that **15 of the 19 gateway-using services notify nothing at all**, so this was never the last mile on every page. Thirteen store tests and seven drawer tests, six mutations, all killed.

- **Discover's page class stops touching the database.** `DiscoverProfileContextStore` takes the last of it: the target roles and their keywords, the compensation target, and the geography the settings narrow the search to - everything a posting is read _against_. Class **653 -> 618**, and `discover.component.ts` no longer injects `DbService` at all. **A duplicate read went with it.** The page called `listSources()` and wrote the result into `DiscoverSourcesService`'s own signal from outside, while that service already has a `reload()` doing exactly that - the same query twice, and a write across a boundary. The bootstrap is now three parallel loads that each own what they read. **The rescan banner's rule moved with the data it compares**: whether the market changed since the scan is a fact about the context, while whether the user dismissed the banner is page state, and the page now combines the two. That comparison is by JSON rather than set membership on purpose, because order is part of what the settings stored. **Discover is not yet free of the gateway**, and the count says so: 46 components became **45**, because `DiscoverSourcesService` still injects `DbService`. It cannot move to `libs/application` as it stands - it raises seven toasts, and telling the user is the app's job. That is the next decision, not an oversight. Sixteen tests, eight mutations, one survived the first pass: requiring **both** ends of a compensation range, which every existing case missed because they set both or neither. Closed with the floor-only case - and only that one, because the parser is positional and takes the first two numbers it finds, so a ceiling without a floor is read as a floor and no markdown produces a `max` without a `min`.

- **Discover's feed and every write that changes it are a store.** `DiscoverFeedStore` owns the rows, the render window and the four writes - load, save, dismiss/undo and clear. Class **679 -> 653**, and the page's remaining gateway calls are down to **three**: the profile, the settings and the source list it bootstraps from. **Filtering and sectioning stayed on the page** on purpose: they read the filter controls the page owns, and they produce what to render rather than what is true. The store answers what the feed contains; the page decides what of it to show. **Two writes mirror in opposite orders, and that is deliberate rather than sloppy.** Saving mirrors **after** the write, because saving is what the user asked for and a failure means it did not happen - showing it saved first would be a lie the toast then contradicts. Dismissal mirrors **before**, because it is a triage gesture over a long list with an undo in reach, and waiting for the database would make the list feel stuck. Both orders now have a test that fails if they swap. `FeedRow` moved to the store, since the store owns the rows, and the page's `discover-feed.ts` re-exports it so the page keeps one name for one type. `discardUnsaved` exists separately from `clear` for the caller that is about to scan anyway: re-reading a feed that is about to be replaced is one round trip for rows nobody sees, and that caller deliberately continues past a failure, so it raises rather than reports. Seventeen tests, eight mutations, all eight dead on the first pass.

- **Discover's scan and its console are a store, and the follow-up work stays inside the scan.** `DiscoverScanStore` owns `scanning`, the console lines and whether the console is open, and runs the scan. Class **687 -> 679** - a small number for a real change, because the method that left was replaced by an orchestration of the same length; what actually moved is three signals and a module. **The follow-up work is a continuation, not something the caller does after `run` returns**, and that is behaviour rather than style: in the version this replaces the feed reload happened inside the same `try`, so the console stayed open across it and a failure there narrated as a failed scan. `view()` reads `scanning`, so releasing it early would show the old feed for a frame. **The store never raises a toast** - telling the user is the app's job, so `run` reports the failure back and the page decides. **`discover-console.ts` did not go to `libs/core`**, and that is the second amendment to `ADR-0005`: it builds dot-padded labels and CSS tone names for a terminal widget, so it is about how something looks, and the domain library is not where that belongs. It is `scan-console.ts` beside the store. The rule is now "a domain rule goes to `libs/core` through the gate; a pure module that formats for one store goes beside that store" - the test is what a module is about, not whether it is pure. Thirteen tests, and eight mutations of which **one survived the first pass**: `collapse()` also stopping the scan, which the toggle test missed because it collapsed when no scan was running. One more was killed only by an unhandled rejection crashing the runner, which is not a kill worth counting, so the assertion that catches it cleanly was added and verified against the mutation in isolation.

- **The application layer exists, and Discover's detail screen is its first tenant.** `libs/application` arrives with `DiscoverDetailStore` rather than empty, and the state of the open job - the description blocks, the detected skills, the raw score, the salary and the verdict - leaves `discover.component.ts` entirely. Class **730 -> 687**; the store is 110 against its 250 budget. **It is component-scoped**, provided by the page: the state of one open job must not survive leaving Discover. That is a different case from `LinkedDocumentsService`, whose known bug is a minutes-long generation outliving its page - the only await here is a single read, and the guard that refuses to write after the user has moved on came with the code and now has a test. **Two page-local pure modules moved to `libs/core` to make it possible at all**, and that is a general consequence of `ADR-0005` the decision itself did not surface: a library cannot import from an app, so state moving into `libs/application` drags the pure modules it calls with it. `discover-detail-scoring.ts` is `libs/core/.../jobs/job-scoring.ts` and `jd-blocks.ts` is `jobs/jd-blocks.ts`; both are rules about jobs and belong beside `job-identity.ts`. Their 40 tests moved intact - the repository total is **1752 either way**, 1419 desktop, 301 core, 16 data, 16 application. **The score is now computed against what the page knew when the user clicked**, passed in as a context object instead of read back off page signals after the await: re-reading signals after an await is the shape of the very race the in-flight guard defends against, and this removes the second half of that problem rather than guarding it twice. Sixteen tests, and eight mutations of which **two survived the first pass**: a band boundary moved by one point, which every existing case missed because they all landed far from 80, and the job title dropped from what is scored, which no case caught because none had a keyword that appears only in the title. Both are closed. **This does not reduce the 46 direct `DbService` injections** - Discover still injects it for the scan, save and dismiss paths, and that count only falls when a page's data access moves in full.

- **Applye gets an application layer, and the file-size campaign folds into it.** `ADR-0005`: page state moves to signal stores in a new `libs/application`, because the campaign was treating a symptom. Page classes reach 700 to 1000 lines since a page is view, state and orchestration at once, and extracting pure helpers does not bring them back - **that was measured twice**, on Profile (stopped at 445/400 by decision rather than technique) and on Discover (890 -> 730, and only while pure logic remained). What is enforced today: a new `type:application` tag with `application -> data, domain, util`, and a **250-line budget** for stores, tighter than the ordinary 400 because a store is the next candidate to become a god-object once the component stops being one. That budget decides the shape of the work - the ratchet refuses a **new** file born over budget, so `jobs.component.ts` at 1050 lines decomposes into several stores by responsibility rather than into one relocated lump. Plain `signal()` and `computed()`, **not** NgRx: `jobs.store.ts` already recorded why, and the reason - a peer range on `@angular/core` gating every Angular major - now applies layer-wide. **The boundary is deliberately not yet closed**: `type:data` stays in `type:app`'s allowlist until no component injects `DbService` (46 do), because flipping it now would fail lint in 46 places at once; until then the rule is held by review, and the changelog says so rather than implying enforcement that does not exist. `db.service.ts` stays as it is and becomes internal to the layer - six lines per method is a mechanical IPC mapping, not complexity, and splitting it would touch imports in files the migration rewrites anyway.

- **Discover's deterministic scoring leaves the page class, and one of its numbers turns out not to mean what it says.** `SKILL_DICT`, `detectSkills` and `computeRawScore` are the zero-token half of the detail screen - which technologies a posting names, and how well it fits the profile - and **none of it had a test**. `discover-detail-scoring.ts` owns them now, beside `discover-location-selection.ts`. **Not in `libs/`**, same reasoning: nothing outside this page needs it. `detectSkills` moved token-for-token and all 45 dictionary entries are unchanged; `computeRawScore`'s three signal reads became arguments, which makes visible an ordering dependency that used to be implicit - the skills have to be detected before the score is computed, or the bonus is taken from the previous job. Class **802 -> 730**, 17 tests over rules that had none. Eight mutations, eight killed, including the two that decide what a posting is read as saying: the whole-word cutoff moving by one character, which makes "MySQL" name SQL as well, and a secondary archetype boosted like a primary one. **Two claims in the code's own comments were wrong and are corrected.** Coverage does not saturate at ten keywords - the _denominator_ does, so matching more than ten pushes coverage above 1 and only the ceiling bounds it. And the clamp is written `20..97`, but the floor is **unreachable**: the formula starts at 30 and every other term is non-negative, so the worst possible posting scores 30. The test pins 30, not 20, so a change that could drop below it fails.

- **Discover's location filter tree leaves the page class, and gets tested for the first time.** The Locations popover keeps one flat set of keys behind three levels of checkbox - a country name (`Germany`) and a city key (`Germany Berlin`) - and derives every level's state from it rather than storing it. That is the only place in Discover where one click has to reason about what its children already are, and **it had no tests at all**: the tri-states, the indeterminate rendering and the cascading add/remove all lived inline on a page whose spec never touched them. `discover-location-selection.ts` now owns `buildRegionGroups`, `countrySelectionState`, `regionSelectionState`, `withCountryToggled` and `withRegionToggled`, beside `discover-location.ts`, which keeps recognising locations - a different job. **Not in `libs/`**: nothing outside this page needs any of it, and a `libs/` public API change is a maintainer decision rather than a refactor. Class **877 -> 802**, and 19 tests cover rules that previously existed only inside method bodies. The two state functions and the tree builder reconstruct token-for-token; **the two toggles do not, and that is deliberate** - the identical seven-line `apply` closure was written out twice, once per level, and is one `applied` helper now. Eight mutations, eight killed, including the two the shape invites: a country reading as fully selected on its own key alone, when the whole point of that key is that it means "including places not listed"; and a partial country clearing instead of completing, which is not what a half-checked box invites.

- **Discover's three filter menus are one component used three times.** Sources, Type and Locations were the same dropdown written out three times over: a ghost trigger carrying a count badge and a chevron, an open signal, a fixed backdrop closing on click and on Escape, a `role="group"` panel, and a foot holding Clear. Only the panel body differed. `DiscoverFilterMenuComponent` is the shell, and **the body arrives as projected content**, which is what makes the boundary small: projected markup compiles in the page's own template scope, so all thirty-odd symbols it binds - `sourceChecked`, `toggleWork`, `regionState`, `toggleCity`, the whole region/country/city tree - never cross it. What is left is `label`, `count`, an optional `footNote` and a `cleared` output. The 35-symbol markup surface that had this filed as "needs a service-owned boundary" was the sum of three different bodies, not one boundary. **Three open signals left the page class** because nothing outside each menu's own markup ever read them. Template **567 -> 484**, stylesheet **748 -> 704**, class 878 -> 877. `__item`, `__expand`, `__region` and `__hint` stay in the page's stylesheet on purpose: the page renders them into the panel, so they keep the page's encapsulation attribute; only the host, `__count`, `__backdrop`, `__panel` and `__foot` moved. **The foot is a generalisation, not a move** - Sources and Type showed one only to hold Clear, Locations always shows one because its hint explains how unmatched countries are grouped, and both fall out of the same two conditions, so all four combinations have a test. Everything else reconstructs token-for-token: substituting each usage's inputs back into the shell reproduces all three original blocks exactly, modulo Prettier moving a `>`. Six mutations, six killed. **One real behaviour change, worth naming**: projected content is created with the page's view even while the menu is closed, so the bodies' bindings now evaluate when they previously did not. Nothing is inserted into the DOM until the menu opens, and every one of those bindings is a pure read.

- **Discover's filter-toolbar controls are hoisted, ahead of the cut that needs them.** The three filter menus - Sources, Type, Locations - are the same dropdown rendered three times, and the shell around them is about to become a component. Two page rules would have stopped matching the moment it did. `.dv-filters .dv-btn` is the toolbar's whole point: it pins every trigger to the query input's 30px height, padding, font size and radius so the bar reads as one control vocabulary instead of three heights.
